# Next 61 publication audit inventory

**All 61 exact-publication audits are complete and coordinator-reviewed:**
**55 recommendations to integrate with recorded limits; six holds.** No active
or pending assignment remains in this fixed pass under the
[commission](./audit-next-61-commission.md). It is separate from the
[completed earlier audit](./unintegrated-audit-inventory.md). None of these 61
candidates was integrated by this pass; recommendations are not integration
authority. Preserve the earlier 13 semantic holds and all six new holds.

Intake selection cutoff: 2026-10-15T06:29:28.176376Z. Origin integration checkpoint:
`26c5bf9df8ca39166fd6bdc39476689dad437cb5`, 70 retained sets. The machine intake
hash and verification method are in the commission. Later Synto and Sift-KG
publications are not assigned in this pass. Source revisions and publication
hash maps below bind audit scope; filesystem timestamps alone do not prove
successful workflow execution. All 61 trace identities were matched, not audited.

**Historical-lead amendment:** the initial literal-URL search missed 17 accounts
already linked by the target checklist, including case variants and moved source
names. Those checklist leads are now included below; they are not assertions of
source equivalence. The immutable intake remains unchanged. The ai-modules
primary report's historical-comparison gap was resolved through a separately
owned supplement; both reports were fully reviewed. No source or publication pin changed.

**Operational acceptance:** [coordinator dispositions](./audit-next-61-operational-holds.md)
retain NAMS and llmwiki-marimo holds and place LLM-Wiki-v3 on hold for the same
unresolved cross-owner write/removal effect. The latter's conditional positive
auditor recommendation remains unchanged in its report. Reviewed inventory
dispositions include coordinator reconciliations; report headlines alone are
not an integration-selection rule.

**Final coordinator verification — 61 reviewed:** all **793 publication files**
still match their complete commissioned maps, including assembled manifests.
Working declarations, completed effects/attempts and method HEADs also match.
All 58 Git source pins remain clean; all three captures retain their declared
hashes. Every nonhistorical machine-intake field and the immutable intake digest
remain unchanged.

All 61 reports, ai-modules' historical supplement and nine coordinator/workshop
documents passed JSON and full-text validation: **71 explicit Markdown targets,
zero failures, warnings, notices or excluded subtrees**. These workshop files
are implicit text; this does not semantically certify their contents.
`git diff --check` and Bash syntax checks for scripts 15–17 also passed.

The **three semantic holds** are ModularRSI (review fail-open and partial-file
claims), JAZ (replay's sequential-invocation requirement), and Supermemory
(tool-payload exclusion qualification; normal SDK reachability unverified and
no disclosure observed). Each requires separately authorized public correction
or supported narrowing, validation and independent review of changed bytes.

The **three operational holds** are NAMS, LLM-Wiki-v3 and llmwiki-marimo.
Their sampled sibling writes/removals are established; whether prior contents
were overwritten is not. No consequential content defect was established for
these three. Resolve the specific ownership/effect question and obtain explicit
authorized acceptance; clean validation alone does not unblock them. Detailed
evidence and bounded conditions remain in the reports, exact-run records and
[operational disposition](./audit-next-61-operational-holds.md).

## Scheduling and review

The parent coordinator owns this inventory. Fresh Sol/medium auditors own only
their named report. At most four active workers; asynchronous first-completion
refill, no nested coordination. Record launch handles before advancing scheduling;
completed reports require full coordinator review before a reviewed disposition.
Do not overwrite partial reports or infer ownership from stale fields after an
interruption.
